2.   RECOGNITION  
A.   TSD Mission Moment—Boulan Park Middle School Effective Teens 
Tonight we honor a special group of students and teachers who have worked to 
hone leadership skills while volunteering their time to help younger students find 
success. The Boulan Park Middle School Effective Teens is made up of 7th and 8th 
grade students who practice the 7 Habits of Highly Effective Teens, participate in 
service learning experiences, and create student-driven Genius Hour projects.  
Students volunteer weekly at Bemis Elementary and serve as mentors to   
elementary students with iPad activities, math, art, writing, science, and reading. 
Effective Teens volunteers learn how to better navigate academic situations and 
how to plan and modify lessons for the youngsters they have in their care. They 
also practice flexibili ty and di fferentiation skills. They analyze and reflect upon 
their experience in a student-led problem-solving debriefing session afterward.  
Genius Hour is a student -driven, project -based learning opportunity. Students 
will spend one to two class periods each wee k researching and working on a 
project that interests them. Genius Hour culminates in student presentations to 
the other Effective Teens classes in the Troy School District at the Genius Hour 
Sharing Event field trip in May. 
At this time, we would like to  call up teachers , Mary Typinski and Michelle Slaviero, 
who teach these classes and lead this impressive group as well as the students 
involved to be recognized. 
________________________________________________ 
Recommended Action:  Invite participants up to congratulate them.

2.       RECOGNITION 
B. Sodexo Healthy High School Challenge 
At this time, we would like to invite Gayle Moran, Director of Food Services 
for Sodexo to recognize two of our high schools for their success with the 
Healthy High School Challenge. 
The Healthy High School Challenge is a national event aimed at promoting 
healthy lunch and snack options in the cafeteria.  High school nutrition 
programs managed by Sodexo around the county competed in the 
challenge, earnin g points for meals and healthy a la carte snacks and 
beverages purchased by students.  The more healthy meals and healthy 
snacks and beverages a school sold during the challen ge, the more points 
they earned, while competing for grant money as prizes.    
Gayle, would you please come forward to explain how our high schools 
fared and honor those who will be receiving grant money tonight? 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Gayle will present checks.

the Troy School District.

success of students and act as the liaison between the

Principal Appreciation Month 
16-017 
The Board recognized All-State athletes for the fall and 
Mr. Dziatczak announced that throughout the State of 
Michigan, February has traditionally been celebrated as 
school and the community it serves, ensuring that parents 
and the community are aware of student and school 
achievements; and, 
WHEREAS,  Troy School District principals are

providing sound leadership and guidance to their staff and 
students; and,  
WHEREAS,  during this month, we join with educators,

awareness of the importance of educational leadership, and 
to recognize and thank all principals working in Troy for 
their exemplary service;

Education officially recognizes February as Principal 
Appreciation Month in Troy and thanks our dedicated  
building leaders for all they do for our students and staff.

TSD Mission Moment – Barnard 
Fourth Graders – Oakland 
County Clean Water Calendar 
Elementary who recently participated in the Oakland 
County Clean Water Calendar Contest.  He shared that they 
showed their passion for protecting local fresh water 
sources by participating in this annual event, which is 
sponsored by the Oakland County Water Resources 
Commission.  He asked Barnard principal, Melanie Morey, 
to come up and introduce the teachers and students who 
participated in this event.  Mrs. Morey invited teachers 
Debbie Rosenman and Nicole Coffer to introduce their 
students and share a bit about the contest. Mrs. Rosenman 
explained that this is part of their first unit in Michigan 
Studies, which focuses on the Great Lakes ecosystem. Each 
year for the past eight years, Barnard fourth graders have 
had their artwork chosen from hundreds of entries 
throughout the county to be featured in the annual 
calendars. After a lesson in Michigan Studies class on the 
importance of protecting the Great Lakes Watershed, all 
fourth grade Barnard students submitted a drawing for the 
contest this year. With over 800 entries in the county,

Jessica Wong, Kaustubh Singh, Sumaiya Rob, Samantha 
Steiner, and Ayaan Syed.

them for their efforts in such an important matter.

PERSONNEL 
Enrollment Projections 
A. 
    Minutes of the January 12, 2016, Board of Education 
None. 
Mr. Dziatczak, Deputy Superintendent for Teaching and 
explained briefly about the data that is collected and 
analyzed for projecting enrollment numbers for public 
school districts. He then reviewed the historical data as 
well as information on schools of choice students in the 
District and the impact schools of choice has on the District 
related to our current level of enrollment.  He talked about 
the three scenarios of projection levels in the report – low, 
most likely and high and then explained how the 
projections are calculated for enrollment using that 
specific formula.   He compared the projected enrollment 
numbers for 2015/2016 to the actual enrollment for this 
year.  He indicated that the District has 98 more students 
this year than the number that was projected.  
There was some conversation amongst board members 
related to the impact enrollment numbers have on the 
budget.   
Facilities Review.  He shared that the Facilities Study Team 
members, who are current and former parents as well as 
community members, have been meeting every Tuesday 
night to look at how district facilities are being used.   The 
team divided up into four groups to study specific areas: 
Attendance Areas, Schools of Choice numbers and 
enrollment projections, Early Childhood and other special 
programs, and Building Capacity issues.   He indicated that 
the group will have a report of their findings for the 
Dr. Philippart said the Board would be looking forward to 
hearing their report.

 7. A.   03.15.16 
7. CURRICULUM 
A.  CONSIDERATION:   Schools Closed to Open Enrollment for  
                                        2016/2017 
Administration has reviewed the enrollment for elementary, middle, and high 
school attendance areas.  Due to the elementary building ’s total student 
enrollment and physical space constraints, we recommend that Bemis and 
Schroeder Elementary Schools be closed to open enrollment.  Our goal  for 
Athens and Troy High  has been to maximize each building’s enrollment which 
has resulted in  both schools being closed to open enrol lment for the past 
several years. Based on current enrollment projections for the 2016/2017 school 
year, it is recommended that Athens and Troy High  be closed to open 
enrollment with the exception of not more than 25  incoming ninth grade 
students who reside in the Troy High School attendance area, but wish to 
attend Athens High School.  Applications for the 25 spots at Athens High School 
will be accepted from March 21-April 1, 2016. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Troy Board of Education adopts a resolution to 
close Bemis Elementary and Schroeder Elementary  School as well as Athens High and 
Troy High School to open enrollment for the 2016/2017 school year, with the exception 
of not more than 25 incoming ninth grade students who reside in the Troy High School 
attendance area , but wish to attend Athens High School.  Students with siblings 
attending Athens and Troy High and currently enrolled in grades 9 – 11 will be allowed 
to open enroll to remain for the 2016/2017 school year.  
Yes: __________ 
No: __________

SUBJECT:       Schools of Choice Recommendations  
 248.823.4000 
                          for the 2016-17 School Year 
 248.823.4013 (fax) 
The following factors were considered in making the recommendations for the Schools of Choice Program for  
2016-2017 school year: 
 Current enrollment 
 Enrollment projections, patterns and trends 
 Numbers and percentages of Schools of Choice students currently in the district 
 Projected number of Schools of Choice siblings who would be age-eligible to enter the district in 2016-2017 
if the decision is made to admit a “Limited” number of kindergarten, first and sixth through eighth grade  
students (Baker Middle School) under Schools of Choice 
 Available elementary classrooms 
 Recommendations from Facilities Study Team 
After analyzing the data and factors referenced above, we recommend that the district: 
 Use Fred Ignatovich’s 1.5 enrollment projection numbers, with his recommended formula (most likely + 
low/2) as the foundation for determining the number of Schools of Choice (SOC) students to admit for the 
2016-2017 school year.  Please note when reviewing “Document 1,” that our “actual” enrollment as of the 
February Count for both kindergarten and first grade students was above both Mr. Ignatovich’s “Most 
Likely” and “Low” enrollment 2015-16 projections. 
 Accept SOC applications for 100 Kindergarten and 30 First Grade students.   Based upon the number of 
applications to kindergarten received for the 2015-2016 school year, we anticipate attracting more than 100 
applicants.  This would allow us to admit 100 kindergartners initially, with the option of admitting additional 
students, if space allows, in the third week of August.  While this is quite close to the opening of school, we 
have found in the past that some families still welcome the opportunity to enroll their child in the Troy 
School District.    
 Limit Schools of Choice kindergarten applicants to the full-day kindergarten program.   
 Accept SOC applications for 15 (total) 6th, 7th and 8th grade students to attend Baker Middle School.  
This will accommodate “Sibling Preference” students for current attending SOC students.  Our estimate for 
enrollment next year approximates to 727.  Accepting 15 applications during the initial application process 
will bring us extremely close to an ideal total population at Baker, while allowing an option to admit 
additional students in August if space permits. 
121
Document 1:  Elementary Enrollment Projections:  Fred Ignatovich’s projection of enrollment (high, average, low and 
most likely) in Kindergarten and First Grade for 2016-2017.   
Document 2:  Elementary Schools of Choice Application History/Retention Rate:  Chart shows the number of Schools 
of Choice students admitted each year and the retention rate, beginning with the 2006-2007 school year. 
Document 3:  Schools of Choice Students by Elementary Buildings:  This chart shows the total number of Schools of 
Choice students currently attending each elementary school.   
Document 4:  Schools of Choice Students District Wide by Grade Level:  This chart shows the total number of Schools 
of Choice students currently in each grade within the district. 
Document 5:  Schools of Choice Students - Elementary Buildings and Grade Level:  This chart shows the number of 
Schools of Choice students in each elementary building by grade on one page.   
Document 6:  Schools of Choice VS Non-Schools of Choice Students:  Data includes building, grade level breakdown, 
subgroup and ethnicity. 
Document 7:  Elementary Enrollment 2015-2016:  This is a chart you have seen multiple times which summarizes the 
total number of students in each section, each grade, in all elementary buildings within the district. 
Document 8:  Schools of Choice Elementary Kindergarten – 5th Grade Projections:  The 2014-2016 chart indicates the 
number of new SOC students per building for the last three years as well as the percentage they represent in the total 
building population. 
Document 9:  Enrollment History without Schools of Choice Students:  This chart indicates what our enrollment 
would be if we had NO SOC students enrolled. 
Document 10:  Projected Schools of Choice Sibling Preference Students for 2016-2017:  This chart indicates the 
number of SOC Sibling Preference students we can expect to place for the 2016-2017 school year.

Sibling Preference Placement for
Kindergarten, 1st Grade and Baker MS Students 
A survey was emailed to current Schools of Choice families requesting information on siblings
 who would be eligible for Kindergarten, 1st Grade and 6th, 7th and 8th Grade students for the 
2016-17 School Year.

The following factors were considered in making the recommendations for the Schools of Choice Program for 
2016-2017 school year:
 Current enrollment
 Enrollment projections, patterns and trends
 Numbers and percentages of Schools of Choice students currently in the district
 Projected number of Schools of Choice siblings who would be age-eligible to enter the district in 2016-2017 if the 
decision is made to admit a “Limited” number of kindergarten, first and sixth through eighth grade  students (Baker 
Middle School) under Schools of Choice
 Available elementary classrooms
After analyzing the data and factors referenced above, we recommend that the district:
 Use Fred Ignatovich’s 1.5 enrollment projection numbers, with his recommended formula (most likely + low/2) as 
the foundation for determining the number of Schools of Choice (SOC) students to admit for the 2016-2017 school 
year.  Please note when reviewing “Document 1,” that our “actual” enrollment as of the February Count for both 
kindergarten and first grade students was above both Mr. Ignatovich’s “Most Likely” and “Low” enrollment 2015-
16 projections.
 Accept SOC applications for 100 Kindergarten and 30 First Grade students.   Based upon the number of 
applications to kindergarten received for the 2015-2016 school year, we anticipate attracting more than 100 
applicants.  This would allow us to admit 100 kindergartners initially, with the option of admitting additional 
students, if space allows, in the third week of August.  While this is quite close to the opening of school, we have 
found in the past that some families still welcome the opportunity to enroll their child in the Troy School District.   
 Limit Schools of Choice kindergarten applicants to the full-day kindergarten program.  
 Accept “Sibling Preference” applications only for siblings of current SOC students (to attend Baker Middle School 
only).  Our current estimates for enrollment next year approximate to 728.  Accepting 15 applications during the 
initial application process will bring us extremely close to an ideal total student population at Baker, while allowing 
an option to admit more students in August if space permits.
      DATE: March 15, 2016
           TO: Dr. Richard Machesky
    FROM: Kris Griffor
                      Mark Dziatczak
SUBJECT:   Schools of Choice Recommendations 
                          for the 2016-17 School Year
The following factors were considered in making the recommendations for the Schools of Choice Program for 2016-2017 school year:
• Current enrollment
• Enrollment projections, patterns and trends
• Numbers and percentages of Schools of Choice students currently in the district
• Projected number of Schools of Choice siblings who would be age-eligible to enter the district in 2016-2017 if the decision is made to admit a “Limited” 
number of kindergarten, first and sixth through eighth grade  students (Baker Middle School) under Schools of Choice
• Available elementary classrooms
• Recommendations from Facilities Study Team
After analyzing the data and factors referenced above, we recommend that the district:
• Use Fred Ignatovich’s 1.5 enrollment projection numbers, with his recommended formula (most likely + low/2) as the foundation for determining the number 
of Schools of Choice (SOC) students to admit for the 2016-2017 school year.  Please note when reviewing “Document 1,” that our “actual” enrollment as of 
the February Count for both kindergarten and first grade students was above both Mr. Ignatovich’s “Most Likely” and “Low” enrollment 2015-16 projections.
• Accept SOC applications for 100 Kindergarten and 30 First Grade students.   Based upon the number of applications to kindergarten received for the 2015-
2016 school year, we anticipate attracting more than 100 applicants.  This would allow us to admit 100 kindergartners initially, with the option of admitting 
additional students, if space allows, in the third week of August.  While this is quite close to the opening of school, we have found in the past that some 
families still welcome the opportunity to enroll their child in the Troy School District.   
• Limit Schools of Choice kindergarten applicants to the full-day kindergarten program.  
• Accept SOC Applications for 15 (total) 6th, 7th and 8th grade students to attend Baker Middle School.  This will accommodate “Sibling Preference” 
students for current attending SOC students.  Our estimate for enrollment next year approximates to 727.  Accepting 15 applications during the initial 
application process will bring us extremely close to an ideal total student population at Baker, while allowing an option to admit additional students in August 
if space permits.

7.     CURRICULUM 
C. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice Section 105 - Elementary  
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts shall pass a 
resolution each school year if they intend  to implement a Schools of 
Choice program for non -resident students who attend another school 
district within Oakland County.  It would be appropriate, for the 2016-17 
school year, for the Board of Education to consider adopting a resolution 
for Schools of  Choice Section 105 for 100 kindergarten and 30 first 
grade non-resident students who attend a school district within Oakland 
County (total students includ e non Oakland County residents  – section 
105c). 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105 of the State School  Aid 
Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a 
limited Schools of Choice program  for the 201 6-2017 school year; for 100 non-
resident kindergarten and 30 non-resident first grade students  who attend a 
school district within Oakland County (total students include non Oakland County 
residents – section 105c). 
Yes: __________ 
No: __________

 7. D. 03.15.16 
7.     CURRICULUM 
D. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice Section 105c - Elementary 
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts shall pass a 
resolution each school year if they intend  to implement a Schools of 
Choice program for non -resident students who attend another school 
district whose county is contiguous with Oakland County .  I t would be 
appropriate, for the 201 6-17 school year, for the Board of Education to 
consider adopting a resolution for Schools of Choice Section 105c for 100 
kindergarten and 30 first grade non-resident students who attend a 
school district  that is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School 
District boundar ies (total student s include Oakland County residents – 
section 105). 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105 c of the State School Aid 
Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a 
limited Schools of Choice program  for the 201 6-2017 school year; for 100 non-
resident kindergarten and 30 non-resident first grade students  who attend a 
school district that is contiguous with the Oakland Intermed iate School District 
boundaries (total students include Oakland County residents – section 105).

7. CURRICULUM 
E. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105 
In accordance with Michigan  state law, school districts  shall pass a resolution 
each school year if they intend to participate in a Schools of Choice program.  It 
is anticipated that seat s will be available for the 20 16-2017 school year for 
grades 6th - 8th at Baker Middle School .  It would be appropriate at this time to 
consider adopting a Schools of Choice section 105  resolution for 15 non-resident 
6th - 8th grade students  who attend a district within Oakland County (total 
students includes non-Oakland county residents – section 105c). 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105  of the State School Aid 
Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a 
Limited Schools of Choice program  for th e 2016-2017 school year for 15 non-
resident 6th - 8th grade students who attend a school district within Oakland 
County for the purposes of attending Baker Middle School  (total students 
includes non-Oakland county residents – section 105c).

at Troy & Athens High S chools, Smith Middle School and Barnard & Schroeder Elementary 
Schools.  The scope of work for this RFP is to perform parking lot replacements/restoration 
and site lighting upgrades at Troy High, Athens, Smith & Schroeder; it also in cluded 
floodplain work at Barnard.  The Athens Site Lighting work will commence this spring and 
the remainder will commence June 20, 2016 with a completion of August 2016.

recommending th e following vendors based on  low bid for their category along with a
